# 518: If You Need Me, I’ll Give It My All!

"Really?"

From the phone came Alder's shocked voice.

Johan had just informed him that Team Plasma might've captured Kyurem.

As the Champion of Unova, Alder was of course familiar with the situation inside Giant Chasm. Still, even a Champion wouldn't actively provoke a legendary Pokémon—especially not after losing his ace.

Fortunately, Kyurem had always been rather reclusive, quietly residing in Giant Chasm without causing trouble.

Because of this, the Unova League had a sort of unspoken understanding with Kyurem, choosing not to disturb it.

They'd even sealed off most information about Kyurem. Fewer than ten people knew the truth.

And now Johan was saying Team Plasma had taken Kyurem?

That was such a huge bombshell, even someone like Alder struggled to process it.

"I didn't see it with my own eyes, but the odds are high," Johan exhaled.

Honestly? It felt great knowing that when the sky falls, there's someone taller to catch it. This—this was why Champions and the Elite Four existed. They weren't just symbols of power—they were pillars people could lean on in times of crisis.

"This is a problem…" Alder muttered.

Johan agreed.

Team Plasma wasn't known for small plays. If they'd actually grabbed Kyurem, it meant they were going all-in.

"Based on Professor Juniper's analysis of Colress's research… he built a machine capable of controlling and amplifying a Pokémon's power—the 'Colress Machine,'" Alder said gravely.

"If they manage to enhance and control Kyurem with it, the consequences…"

Kyurem was terrifying as-is. Enhanced Kyurem?

"It probably… hasn't happened yet," Johan offered.

"Oh? Why?"

"The Colress Machine can empower and manipulate Pokémon—but not every Pokémon is easily controlled. And it doesn't happen instantly."

"You're saying Colress may not be able to control Kyurem?"

"I think that's the likelier scenario. Otherwise, wouldn't Team Plasma already be acting a lot more… bold?"

"Good point."

If they had Kyurem under control, they'd be flaunting it—not hiding.

Since the Unova League destroyed the P2 Lab, Team Plasma had been retreating. The League managed to seize some of their slower-to-evacuate bases, but most had slipped away.

Their reaction alone was proof enough.

Alder had just shared this info earlier on the call.

"Looks like we've got to act fast…"

Strike before they gain control of Kyurem. Stop them, free Kyurem.

He hung up.

"The Unova stuff… I'll leave that to their Champion."

Johan felt like he already had enough on his plate.

He'd helped Keldeo, and now he needed to figure out what to do about the Genesects.

But the big issue now—was where those Genesects had gone.

"Johan."

The wide open plains stretched under a setting sun. The golden light bathed the valley in warm hues.

Out on the grassy field—

Ceruledge, Tinkaton, Dragonite, Urshifu, and Larvitar were all learning from the three Swords of Justice and Keldeo.

The Swords' teachings weren't limited to swordplay. They trained Keldeo using rivers, grasslands, rocky forests—all kinds of terrain.

Their techniques—Swords Dance, Aura Sphere, close combat maneuvers—were all worth learning.

Cobalion, the leader among them, exuded a natural charisma. Wild Pokémon respected it, even the more brutish ones.

Ceruledge especially admired this—it was the kind of aura it aspired to have.

At the moment, Cobalion stood beside Johan, gazing out toward the distant valley.

"Hmm?"

"Thank you."

Johan smiled, patting Cobalion's armored foreleg. "You've said that already."

"No."

Cobalion shook its head. "This thanks is for helping Keldeo."

Its gaze fell on Keldeo, who was being ridden like a horse by Larvitar—who gleefully shouted "Red Hare!" over and over while bouncing on its back.

Johan twitched at the sight.

Larvitar, buddy… can you not call someone's child a steed to their parent's face?

Still, Cobalion didn't seem to mind. It just sighed gently.

"We've always trained Keldeo. But to avoid making it arrogant, we never gave it true recognition. And in doing so, we overlooked its pride."

"You—and your partners—helped it realize the weight of the sword, what it truly means to be a Sword of Justice."

A sword is a weapon. But as Swords of Justice, theirs were always meant to protect the weak.

Just like they once stood between humans and Pokémon in war, and now stood as ambassadors negotiating with Kyurem for wild Pokémon…

To the Swords, the weight of the blade depended on the resolve to protect.

That's why Cobalion insisted on thanking Johan again.

"It would've figured it out eventually," Johan said humbly.

"Yes, but it could've taken years… decades… maybe longer."

Cobalion slowly lowered its head. From its forehead, a metallic horn extended.

This was the sword. The symbol of the Swords of Justice.

And on its tip—

Was a dark red, fist-sized shard.

Huh?

Johan blinked.

"Is that…?"

Instinctively taking it, his eyes widened.

"A fragment of the Fist Plate?!"

"You recognize it?"

Now it was Cobalion's turn to be surprised.

It explained:

"We found it long ago—when the meteor fell, while protecting wild Pokémon."

Wait a second…

Did Arceus come to Unova when the meteor fell?

He wasn't Rayquaza. What would Arceus want with a meteor?

Then again—

That meteor held the seeds of Kyurem, Zekrom, and Reshiram. And all it left behind was Giant Chasm? That was a bit small.

The meteor in Hoenn that formed Sootopolis City made a whole crater lake.

And that was just a regular meteor. This one birthed three legendaries…

"This fragment doesn't serve us much anymore," Cobalion said. "But it might be useful to you—or your Pokémon."

Johan pursed his lips.

First Lugia had randomly pulled out a fragment of the Earth Plate.

Now Cobalion was offering a piece of the Fist Plate.

At this rate… Rayquaza, Giratina, Dialga, Palkia—surely they had a few fragments stashed somewhere, right?

Tsk.

Guess their friendship level wasn't high enough yet. Couldn't mooch off them.

Still, Plate fragments? You could never have too many.

"Then I'll accept… reluctantly," Johan smirked, pocketing the shard.

Buzz—

For some reason, his aura stirred—becoming energized and vibrant.

Wait.

He was originally going to give the Fist Plate fragment to Urshifu.

But now… even his own aura was reacting?

Aura, as in, Fighting-type energy.

His progress in aura had slowed to a crawl. Even injecting it into the Rainbow Wing barely made a difference now. The Wing always gave off a "that's enough" vibe.

But this Plate shard had stirred his aura back to life.

One question, though.

If he could use a Plate fragment—was he still human?

"…Am I becoming the ultimate protagonist?"

"One more thing."

After gifting the shard, Cobalion visibly relaxed—but spoke again.

"Go ahead."

"Those five who attacked Kyurem—the ones who attacked you…"

"Right, the Genesects. Pokémon from 300 million years ago. Resurrected from fossils, then altered by science."

"I see…" Cobalion's gaze darkened.

"I sensed sorrow and pain from them."

Sorrow and pain…

"If… if possible—could you help them?" Cobalion asked.

Their eyes met.

Cobalion had never truly trusted humans—not since the wars long ago.

But Johan had changed that.

Johan raised his head, drew a deep breath, and smiled:

"You might not believe this, but even before meeting Keldeo—I was already looking for them. Already searching for ways to help."

"If it's within my power—I'll help."

Cobalion continued to stare at him, moved.

It knew Johan wasn't lying.

Because Johan had helped Keldeo. Had helped them.

His aura confirmed it—his words were true.

A human, aiding Pokémon in need. A human whose bond with his Pokémon made others envious.

In that moment, the Sword of Justice saw something radiant in Johan.

A light of justice.

So bright, it made Cobalion shield its eyes.

This man… was truly worthy of respect.

Cobalion declared sincerely:

"If you ever need us—we will give it our all!"

# 519: Pokémon Sanctuary

Unova Region, Nimbasa City.

After parting ways with the Swords of Justice and Keldeo, Johan arrived in one of Unova's major cities—Nimbasa City.

Modeled after New York City in America, Nimbasa's architecture was nearly a carbon copy in many places. Especially its central park—the Pokémon Sanctuary.

This man-made Pokémon paradise in the heart of a bustling city was lush with vegetation, shaded by green trees, and surrounded by flowing water where Pokémon frolicked freely.

Even Johan had to admit, building such a sanctuary amidst a jungle of steel and glass was a real blessing for wild Pokémon.

This was also the very location the Genesects tried to reclaim as their new home in the movie Genesect and the Legend Awakened.

Not just because of the Pokémon Sanctuary itself, but because in the flowing waters of the park bloomed a lotus-like flower known as Aurus—said to be the oldest flower species on Earth, with origins tracing back some 300 million years.

That's why the Genesects chose this spot.

Aurus reminded them of home.

Johan wasn't sure where the Genesects had gone—but he figured they might be searching for traces of their ancient homeland, guided by memory.

Of course, after 300 million years of tectonic shifts and time, that home no longer existed.

So if they ended up here at the Sanctuary, it wouldn't be surprising.

When Johan arrived, though, he didn't see the Genesects. Nor did he see many people—since the sanctuary was still under reconstruction. It was currently closed off to the public. Johan was allowed in only due to his status as an international police inspector.

Accompanying him as a volunteer tour guide was Nurse Joy.

"Mr. Johan, this is the Pokémon Sanctuary. Construction isn't quite finished, but Pokémon have already started living here," Nurse Joy said cheerfully.

"If they haven't returned, and there's no trace of them, then odds are they've found another place to build their home."

Which—honestly—was a good thing for both Johan and the Genesects.

Living far from humans, building a new home, surviving peacefully... what more could anyone ask for?

"Come on out."

Standing by a lake at the front of the Sanctuary, Johan called out his team.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales instantly claimed a sunny stone, curled up atop it for a nap. A thin layer of frost quickly spread over the rock, and a light mist formed around her, refracting sunlight into a dazzling rainbow aura.

After absorbing so much of Kyurem's Ice-type energy, Ninetales' powers had grown considerably. But it would take time to fully master and wield that energy with finesse.

"Ka-kaah!"

"Cha!"

"Draaa!"

"Garruu!"

"Goka!"

"Yoji... Yoji~~!"

Larvitar stood atop a wobbly stone, teetered, and plopped down hard on its backside. Watching this, Johan again questioned whether Uxie had really bestowed "the Wisdom of the Lake" to the right Pokémon...

Still, the team quickly blended in with the Sanctuary's wild residents.

Spring-form Deerling munched on lush grass in the meadows. Pidove and Tranquill glided beneath the sanctuary's artificial dome, almost as if patrolling their new home.

Caterpie and Wurmple nibbled on leaves in the trees. Silcoon and Cascoon dangled by silk threads. Shroomish hid in the underbrush.

Kricketune led a musical show with Audino backup vocals under a tree, while Ludicolo danced joyously. A chorus line of Bellossom twirled gracefully, putting on a real forest performance.

"Draaa~"

Dragonite zoomed skyward, racing the Pidove—who promptly got tossed around like leaves in a storm. But instead of getting mad, the Pidove seemed thrilled and even started following Dragonite like he was their boss, flying laps under the dome with him.

Johan saw a few Pidove about to pass out from exhaustion...

"Goka!"

Ceruledge strode toward a sleeping Houndoom and Persian—clearly the top dogs of the sanctuary's social ladder.

As a former top 'enforcer' of a Day Care, Ceruledge could sense the aura of leaders. It sat down facing the two. Nearby, a few Houndour pups played. The older duo opened their eyes to meet Ceruledge's unwavering stare.

Shing!

Ceruledge drew its sword, igniting it with ghostly blue flames.

Houndoom bared its fangs, flames flickering between clenched teeth—until it truly felt Ceruledge's firepower, whimpered, and bowed its head.

Persian, less convinced, rose slowly, its ruby-like gem glowing ominously.

Clank, clank—

Ceruledge's armor creaked as it brought forth a golden cannon arm.

Persian: Σ(дlll)

Staring down the barrel, Persian gulped and gently placed a paw on the cannon.

Hey, let's just chat. No need to go full military parade.

Ceruledge looked back at Johan, gave an "OK" hand sign.

"Goka!"

Territory secured.

Johan: "…"

"Garruu."

"Yoji."

Urshifu and Larvitar crouched by the lake. Beneath the waterfall-fed water, Water-types stirred.

"Garruu~~" (Watch big bro snag a big one!)

"Yoji!" (Go for it, Coach!)

Splash!

But before Urshifu could act, a giant crocodile face lunged from the depths.

"Cha-roo!!"

A Feraligatr!

Ceruledge's eyes locked on immediately.

Another challenger...

But this time, Urshifu was ready. As the water surged, it calmly raised a fist and dispersed the waves into a neat orb.

"Cha-roo?"

Feraligatr's plan to intimidate clearly backfired.

Moments later, the three—Urshifu, Feraligatr, and Larvitar—were sitting together like old drinking buddies.

Feraligatr, though... was devastated.

Not only had it been totally overpowered, but somehow that little Larvitar had a grip like steel?!

As for Tinkaton—

After wandering the Sanctuary and finding no giant birds to chase, she aimlessly explored the grasslands and ran into a swarm of Durant.

They were instantly drawn to the metallic shine of her hammer.

"Cha?"

Feigning cluelessness, Tinkaton held out her hammer to the Durants.

Curious?

Durants exchanged glances—and couldn't resist. They crowded in.

Minutes later…

Tinkaton had a pile of metal loot, and the Durants looked like obedient minions, shuttling metal from underground to her feet.

If you didn't know better, you'd think she was their queen.

In a shaded corner, a small Pokémon with gemlike eyes peeked out.

Curious, yet timid, Spiritomb watched the others play.

But it was too shy to join.

Until—

"Ka-kaah?"

A voice behind it.

Spiritomb froze. Slowly turned—and saw Gengar, sunglasses on, waving like a cool uncle.

"Ka-kaah~~(▼O▼)/"

Big boss energy.

Spiritomb trembled. Sweat beaded. It turned to flee—

—but Gengar gently stopped it and handed over a glowing Pokéblock.

The aroma overwhelmed Spiritomb. Its gem-eyes went wide.

Gulp—

Gengar stuffed the treat in its hands.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Let's be friends. I love making new friends.

Gengar's mischievous eyes twinkled.

He knew fellow Ghost-types. Spiritomb probably knew this sanctuary better than anyone.

Perfect for scouting rare treasures.

I'm a genius, Gengar grinned.

But Gengar didn't realize—"friendship" meant something profound to a shy, solitary Spiritomb.

It clutched the Pokéblock tightly.

Then nodded.

Then disappeared into the shadows.

"Ka-kaah?"

Huh?

Gengar scratched his head.

Too much charm? Scared it off?

But then—

Spiritomb returned. Arms full of glittering gemstones, crystals, and sparkling dust.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar blinked. Pointed at the gems. Then at himself.

For me?

Spiritomb nodded solemnly.

Friend.

"Ka-kaah…"

Even cheeky Gengar felt a little guilty.

These were gems, after all.

Was a single Pokéblock really a fair trade?

So he offered a whole box.

Spiritomb's eyes nearly popped.

Johan watched, rubbing his chin.

Spiritomb, a Ghost/Dark-type like Larvitar, loved gemstones.

In Kalos, it was even a predator of Carbink.

Johan wasn't thinking about Gengar and Spiritomb though.

He was thinking—

Maybe building a Pokémon sanctuary like this… could be something worth doing.

A place for Pokémon in need. With themed habitats. Zones suited for all species.

The only problem was—it'd cost a fortune.

"You really did bond with every Pokémon here," Nurse Joy said in amazement.

From top dogs like Houndoom to nobodies like Spiritomb—Johan's team got along with everyone.

"Bonded by battle," Johan smirked.

Just don't wreck the whole sanctuary while you're at it.

"Are you thinking of building your own?" Nurse Joy asked.

Johan had said he was here to "study and reference" the design.

"Well, I am an advisor for Devon Corporation," he coughed. "Devon has plans for simulated habitats. I'm just gathering ideas."

Nurse Joy nodded.

"Nimbasa's Sanctuary is good, but if you're serious, you should check out Blueberry Academy. Their ecological dome inspired a lot of what we did here."

"Blueberry Academy, huh? Sounds worth a look."

Just then, Johan's phone rang.

"Excuse me."

Nurse Joy left to check on some wild Pokémon with Chansey. Johan stepped over to Ninetales' stone and answered.

"Johan, where are you?" It was Cynthia's voice.

"Nimbasa."

A pause. Then Caitlin's voice cut in.

"With all that's happened in Unova…"

Clearly, Caitlin—being from Unova and a major League figure—was aware of recent events.

The discovery of P2 Lab had forced Team Plasma into hiding.

They still couldn't control Kyurem, so they were being cautious.

But once they could?

A clash with the League was inevitable.

Especially with the World Youth Masters Tournament coming up.

The League couldn't afford to look bad in front of visiting champions.

"We'll be in Nimbasa tonight," Cynthia said.

"Perfect."

With Cynthia and Caitlin here, Johan felt more confident facing the Genesects again.

As he hung up, his phone buzzed again.

"Jeez, busy day."

This time, it was a message from Porygon-Z:

[Gengar's Little Fanboy]: They're heading your way.

Genesects!

They were coming.

They were on the move again.

Johan's expression turned serious.

Their wounds were probably healed by now.

Could he and his team still hold out?

[Grain in the Field]: ETA?

If he had time, he could call Alder.

"Message received, Loto!"

RotomDex lit up with coordinates.

[Who Am I]: One hour.

…What?!

# 520: Johan: Get Them!

Unova Region, Nimbasa City – Pokémon Sanctuary.

"One hour?"

"Miss Joy, wait—"

Johan quickly informed Nurse Joy that the Genesects might be coming.

Her eyes widened in shock.

She hadn't expected such powerful legendary Pokémon to target the Sanctuary.

But thankfully…

"With you here, Mr. Johan, we're lucky," Joy said, patting her chest with relief.

Johan's cheek twitched.

You really overestimate me…

The last time he faced the five Genesects—injured and weakened, no less—he barely held his ground. That was only possible because they were fighting in Kyurem's Giant Chasm, where Ninetales had a massive home-field advantage and they managed to isolate the Genesects one by one.

Now?

They were in the open, natural terrain. Ninetales was stronger, sure, but the Genesects would be prepared. Isolating them again would be near impossible.

Facing all five at once under coordinated command?

Johan didn't feel confident.

But to Nurse Joy…

This was the man who punched out Dialga and Palkia, who rode Rayquaza. If a legendary Pokémon showed up, surely Johan had a plan.

"Miss Joy, please lead everyone away," Johan said.

Some of the Sanctuary's wild Pokémon were strong—

Feraligatr, Houndoom, Persian…

But just strong wasn't enough against the Genesects.

"Understood."

Joy nodded without hesitation.

She wasn't about to interfere with Johan's actions.

Of course, the wild Pokémon weren't thrilled.

This was their home, after all. Even if it was temporary, they didn't want to leave.

"Goka!"

Ceruledge stepped up.

At Johan's signal, Ceruledge offered the wild Pokémon a feast—with delicious "PY" Pokéblocks made for flavor.

Instantly, the wild Pokémon flocked over.

With Joy, Chansey, Ceruledge, and others helping, they began gathering the Sanctuary's residents.

It took time—this place was huge, and many Pokémon lived here.

Eventually, outside on the grassy fields, a crowd of wild Pokémon gathered.

"You… need to run!"

A young voice echoed in Johan and Ceruledge's minds.

Hm?

Johan immediately looked at Ceruledge.

"Goka!"

Ceruledge nodded.

It was that Genesect!

Back in the Giant Chasm, Johan had sent Ceruledge to fight the one with the Ice Drive, hoping to connect with it.

In the movie, that same Genesect had doubted its mission, was persuaded by Ash, and fought to protect other Pokémon.

Ceruledge had indeed bonded with it somewhat.

And later, Johan learned the one who had sent the distress signal to Porygon-Z wasn't all the Genesects—just this one.

It had done so unconsciously.

But hadn't Porygon-Z said they'd arrive in an hour?

It's only been… what, thirty-something minutes?

They sped up?

"Draaa~"

Dragonite, hovering in the sky, pointed off in the distance.

A black dot appeared—growing larger and closer.

It was them.

The five Genesects, flying in tight formation!

"They're here!"

"That fast?!" Joy gasped.

The wild Pokémon looked confused.

Wasn't this supposed to be a picnic?

Whoosh whoosh whoosh—

In the blink of an eye, the Genesects were overhead.

The red leader scanned the area, immediately spotting Johan and Gengar.

Its eyes glowed red.

"Enemy. Eliminate."

Before the order was even given—

"That's… it!"

The Ice Drive Genesect spotted the Aurus flowers blooming in the lake.

Seeing them, the Genesects grew excited.

Home.

A memory of home!

Their first real sign of it in millennia.

The red Genesect's eyes dimmed slightly.

Protect… home.

It landed.

The others followed, forming a line behind it.

The Ice Drive Genesect looked toward Ceruledge.

"Leave. Quickly."

The red one growled at it.

It recoiled and said no more.

Then it turned toward Johan and the Pokémon present, crouched low, revealing its back cannon.

Bzzzt—

Energy gathered.

"This is our home! No one comes near! Or… you'll be eliminated!"

"Eliminate!" ×4

The others lifted their cannons in unison, taking aim.

It was clear—they didn't want to fight.

But if the Pokémon didn't back down, they would.

Everyone could hear the psychic message—they understood.

Johan and his team stayed still.

But the wild Pokémon?

They stirred, angry and defiant.

Especially Feraligatr, Houndoom, and Persian.

"Charoo!!"

Feraligatr roared first, arms wide, standing firm with zero fear.

This was their home.

He'd been friendly with Urshifu and Larvitar—but these weren't allies.

These were invaders.

Houndoom and Persian stepped up beside him.

Seeing that, the red Genesect's eyes flared crimson.

"Eliminate."

It gave the warning.

But the Pokémon stood their ground.

The attack came.

Feraligatr fired Hydro Pump, Houndoom let loose Flamethrower, Persian unleashed Hyper Beam.

But combined, their power still paled in comparison to the red Genesect's tech cannon.

"Armarouge!"

Johan's sharp command rang out.

They weren't unprepared.

Armarouge had already transformed. Its golden cannon gleamed, fire swirling inside.

Johan had originally planned to retreat, wait for Alder, Cynthia, or Caitlin to arrive, and avoid confrontation.

But the Genesects came early.

There was no more avoiding a clash.

"Full power! Armor Cannon!" Johan shouted.

Aura power, infuse!

His eyes glowed blue, his Aura flowing into Armarouge.

The cannon flared, red armor blazing—then turned gold.

Life Flame ignited.

And not just that—

Psychic energy!

As a Fire/Psychic type, Armarouge poured its psychic power into the blast too.

The sheer energy made Nurse Joy step back, her mouth agape.

This is just an Armor Cannon?!

Even real cannons aren't this wild!

BOOM!!

Flames joined the others' attacks.

BOOMMMM!!

A thunderous explosion.

The shockwave knocked over smaller Pokémon. Silcoon and Cascoon rolled away like bowling balls.

As the smoke cleared, heat lingered.

The red Genesect stared ahead.

Armarouge's golden cannon still smoked. A charred trench stretched from its feet to the blast zone.

The cannon shot—was blocked!

The red Genesect remembered the Giant Chasm.

"Formation!"

The five Genesects rose into the air.

They spun, forming a circular rotation—like a revolver—each gathering power.

They weren't letting themselves be isolated again.

"All lifeforms: Evacuate. Eliminate!"

"Eliminate! Eliminate! Eliminate! Eliminate!"

"Wait!"

Johan stepped forward, finally seizing the opening.

He stopped Feraligatr and the others.

Facing the Genesects, he shouted:

"I know where your home is! I can take you there!"

What?

Four of the Genesects showed surprise.

Really?

"You lost your home—so you plan to steal someone else's? I can help you find it. I'm the one who received your distress signal!"

He held up his phone.

"Is it true?" the Ice Drive Genesect asked.

"Humans. Untrustworthy. Eliminate," the red one growled.

"It could be true… maybe we—"

But the Ice Drive Genesect's eyes flared red.

Mind controlled—again!

"Humans. Must be eliminated."

Johan cursed inwardly.

No room for diplomacy?

Just like the movie, the red Genesect used its authority to override the others—refusing to admit its mistakes, even against Mewtwo.

So be it.

Johan's eyes narrowed.

"Get them!" he snapped.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar hurled a massive Shadow Ball straight at the five.

At the same time—

Clink clink clink—

Johan whipped out a red chain.

If it worked on Dialga and Palkia, it could work on Genesects too.

# 521: Mewtwo: Leave It to Me

Clink clink clink—

The red chain shot out, aimed at the red Genesect leader. If Johan could capture it, the remaining four Genesects wouldn't be an issue, since the leader could control the rest.

But—

Whether it was due to the Genesects developing resistance from having been controlled by Colress in the past, or sensing the threat from the chain, the red Genesect leader reacted immediately.

"Scatter!"

In an instant—

It shifted into its saucer form and dodged the red chain with blistering speed.

Extreme Speed.

Normally, Genesect couldn't learn this move—but this red Genesect was an exception.

Its speed rivaled even Mewtwo's Mega-Evolved form.

Their speed duels had a vibe reminiscent of The Flash.

That's why the movie was called "Genesect and the Legend Awakened"—it wasn't just for show.

Its movement was so fast that ordinary people couldn't even perceive it—only a fleeting red blur remained.

Luckily, Johan, using Aura, could barely track it.

"Draaa?"

Dragonite, standing beside Johan, looked up, its gaze following the leader's path. Its stubby wings began flapping.

Johan quickly adjusted his aim.

Since he couldn't catch the leader—

Clink clink clink!

The red chain snapped onto the Ice Drive Genesect, pausing its movement for just a moment—thanks to the emotional resonance embedded in the chain's power.

Even if this red chain wasn't the perfect one used on Dialga or Palkia, it was more than enough to hold a regular Genesect.

Especially with Gengar's Shadow Ball stunning some of the other Genesects.

"Filthy human! Trying to control us again!"

The red Genesect roared in fury.

It remembered the dark days trapped in those chambers—its body and will controlled, a weapon of war.

Even against the terrifying Kyurem, they were ordered never to retreat.

Every day brought the agony of so-called "enhancements."

That's why the red leader hated humans so much—because of Colress and Team Plasma.

If Kyurem hadn't frozen and shattered their control devices, they never would've escaped.

But now—

Another human was trying to control it and its comrades again.

All that rage erupted.

Buzz—

The red Genesect sliced through the air in a crimson streak, charging straight at Johan.

"Draaa!!"

Prepared, Dragonite launched upward, shrouded in swirling white and red energy—

Outrage, engage!

Extreme Speed vs. Extreme Speed!

CRASH!

The two collided head-on.

The red Genesect was momentarily surprised—this chubby-looking Pokémon, with those tiny wings, matched its speed?

Still not enough!

It surged past Dragonite, speeding again toward Johan.

Eliminate the human!

Dragonite's eyes narrowed.

I won't let you hurt Johan!

"DRAAAAA!!"

With a piercing cry, white energy twisted with green—his speed spiked even higher.

Like an arrow shot from a bow.

CRASH!

He intercepted the red Genesect again.

All this happened in just seconds.

Meanwhile, the Ice Drive Genesect, bound by the red chain, trembled. Johan could feel its confusion and yearning—for home.

To Genesects, home was like a mother—nurturing and sacred. That's why they clung so hard to the sight of the Aurus flowers.

"Destroy them!"

The red leader issued another command.

The other Genesects—save the one bound—glowed red, forming a rotating revolver formation.

High-tech cannons charged!

"Everyone, take cover!" Johan shouted.

"Oowoo~!"

Ninetales summoned snowflakes and barriers, but—

BOOM BOOM BOOM BOOM!!

A barrage of beams exploded downward.

Even Pokémon that weren't directly hit were blown away by the shockwaves.

This time, Feraligatr and the others didn't charge blindly.

They'd seen the power difference now.

So they scattered, hiding as Johan signaled.

Johan retreated with his team—and the Ice Drive Genesect—to a hidden area.

He could feel the Pokémon's helplessness and hope.

"Do you want to go home?" Johan asked with Aura.

"Yes."

Though affected by the red chain, it could still respond.

"I'll help you find it," Johan promised.

The Genesect met his gaze.

In its eyes: solemn trust.

Maybe it was Aura. Maybe it was the chain letting their feelings connect.

Or maybe—

It was because Johan answered the distress call it sent.

And because he said: "Just because you lost your home doesn't mean you should take someone else's."

The Genesect nodded deeply.

"Can your leader control you?" Johan asked suddenly.

It nodded.

"The leader... was first. I... youngest."

Its speech was halting, but Johan understood.

The red Genesect was the first Colress revived—from the oldest fossil. It underwent the most modifications.

And so—

It bore the most trauma and hatred toward humans.

Before Johan could ask more—

"They... found you!" the Ice Drive Genesect warned.

Three red-eyed Genesects loomed above, cannons aimed.

The Ice Drive Genesect immediately turned its cannon toward them in defense.

"Oowoo!!"

Ninetales dropped another aurora screen.

Armarouge charged his cannon.

Urshifu and Tinkaton lunged left and right toward the attackers.

Dragonite streaked back into position.

BOOM!!

The world exploded into light and thunder.

But—it was a distraction.

The red leader burst through, heading straight for Johan!

The three others had just created a diversion.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar, no longer grinning, rose from Johan's shadow to confront the leader.

You can do anything—but you can't touch Johan.

Ghost energy surged beneath Gengar's feet—its aura swelled.

"Yoji!"

"Loto!"

Larvitar and Rotom stood by its sides.

Even Shuppet, Gengar's newest friend, gritted its teeth and stepped forward, arms wide.

No one noticed the figure descending from the skies—

A Pokémon, cloaked in unimaginable Psychic power, sweeping over the Sanctuary.

Humanoid, cat-like, silvery-white with a purple tail and belly, nerves extending from its back—

It was Mewtwo.

Its eyes locked onto Johan and the red Genesect.

Seeing the attack, Mewtwo's gaze sharpened.

Its three fingers splayed—

A glowing orb formed.

With a flick of its wrist, the orb launched.

Psychic power rippled across the battlefield.

BOOM!!!

The blast hit behind Johan. Shockwaves tore outward, but a barrier blocked the damage.

Dust and debris scattered.

"Huh?"

Johan blinked in shock.

He had known the red Genesect was behind him. He trusted Gengar to protect him while he tried to sway the Ice Drive Genesect.

But now—

Someone else had intervened?

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar tilted his head.

As the dust cleared, a silver figure came into view—

Gripping the red Genesect tightly—

"Mewtwo?!"

Johan's pupils shrank.

Why is Mewtwo here?!

Was the "one hour" not referring to the Genesects—but to Mewtwo's arrival?

Now that he thought about it—

He'd known Mewtwo for months. They'd talked. But this was the first time he'd seen Mewtwo in person.

That aura pressure… was insane.

Even Gengar tensed.

This thing… was even scarier than the Genesects!

Then Mewtwo looked toward Johan and Gengar.

"Ka-kaah! (▼皿▼)"

Gengar bared his fangs.

It didn't know this one, so it stayed cautious.

No one touches Johan.

But Mewtwo nodded slightly to Johan.

Johan placed a hand on Gengar's shoulder.

"Gengar, it's okay. He's not an enemy."

"Ka-kaah?"

Really?

Gengar scratched his head, side-eyeing Johan.

When did you befriend a monster like that?

He had no idea.

Johan just told Mewtwo through Aura:

"He's stubborn. And strong."

Stubborn?

Strong?

Mewtwo looked at the red Genesect.

"I'll handle it," the voice echoed in Johan's mind.

BOOM!!

Mewtwo's Psychic power surged.

The red Genesect was blown away.

Mewtwo didn't hold back—its figure flashed as it gave chase.

In the movie, Mewtwo had fought the red Genesect to a standstill—out of mercy.

But not now.

This Mewtwo was freshly awakened—at its peak.

Caged for so long, its battle instincts were razor-sharp.

Even without trying, Mewtwo was terrifying.

With the red leader gone, Johan turned to the remaining three Genesects—

Smiling.

The Genesects shivered.

That smile…

Why did it feel so evil?

# 522: I Am Mewtwo

"You don't want these flowers destroyed either, do you?"

Huh?

As soon as Johan said that, he felt... a little awkward. Something about the phrasing felt off.

But he had to admit, it was effective. Holding that Aurus flower in his hand clearly gave the remaining three Genesects pause.

At the same time, the wild Pokémon that lived in the Pokémon Sanctuary had gathered again, watching the three Genesects with clear hostility.

"Goka!"Armarouge stepped forward and signaled for everyone to stay calm.

After witnessing the power of the Genesects—and that of Johan and his team—the wild Pokémon of the sanctuary gradually lowered their guard.

"Everyone..."

The Ice Drive Genesect also stepped forward, trying to help Johan talk them down.

"This is home for all of us. No one would stop you from staying, but driving others out to take over their home—would you really make everyone lose theirs, too?"

Johan's voice echoed in their minds.

"Look around—look at these Pokémon!"

"Ge—no... Ge—no..."

They saw the ruined grasslands, the muddied lake, a Deerling licking its wounds, and Feraligatr, Houndoom, and Persian glaring at them with animosity.

This wasn't what they wanted.

Though their leader had said they'd build a new home, they had never meant to hurt anyone—just clear obstacles.

"Ge—no! Ge—no!"The Ice Drive Genesect continued pleading.

But then—

The voice of the red Genesect leader returned in their minds.

"What are you doing? Finish the purge!"

"Leader..." the Ice Drive Genesect tried to argue.

"Leader, maybe we—"

"Leader, they have the flower—"

The other three tried to speak, but the leader didn't care. It immediately took control again:

"Eliminate them all!"

BZZZZZ—

The three Genesects' eyes glowed red, once more under their leader's control—except the Ice Drive one, which remained affected by the red chain.

Johan winced.

Seriously?

That red Genesect kept screaming about not wanting to be controlled by humans—and here it was, happily controlling its own kind.

Johan looked up.

No sign of Mewtwo or the red Genesect.

But remembering that Mewtwo could hear certain "special voices," Johan reached out with his Aura:

Mewtwo?

A pause of a second or two—

Then Mewtwo's voice came into his mind.

"What is it?"

Johan gritted his teeth: "Beat the crap out of it!"

"…Understood."

——

In downtown Newt City, Mewtwo and the red Genesect hovered high in the air, facing off.

Mewtwo had actually been considering trying to reason with it—but Johan's message changed that.

"Sorry, then."

As a powerful Psychic-type, Mewtwo could sense the fury raging within the Genesect—almost identical to what it felt during its own birth.

Better to subdue it first.

BOOM!!

Mewtwo's psychic power exploded.

Sensing the threat, the red Genesect raised its cannon—

BZZZZZ—

A high-tech beam surged forward, but Mewtwo's psychic shield held it back.

The impact, though, slammed Mewtwo down to the ground.

"Obstacle. Eliminate."

Rather than flee, the red Genesect dove after Mewtwo at top speed.

CRASH!!

The street exploded. Pedestrians and cars screeched to a halt, stunned.

The red Genesect arrived at the crater, arms raised, firing twin pale green beams—

But closer inspection revealed them to be concentrated sonic waves.

Bug Buzz!

Colress had engineered these creatures for battle—like Mewtwo, they were no slouches.

Bug-type beats Psychic-type. Basic type advantage.

But just then—

Crack crack crack—

The broken ground split further.

As Bug Buzz closed in through the mist—

BOOM!!

The earth exploded. Rocks flew everywhere—blocking the attack.

But as they flew toward nearby civilians and Pokémon—

They froze midair. Caught by some invisible force. Then gently dropped.

Everyone stared in awe.

In the dispersing fog stood a figure, one arm raised, holding a glowing, electric-blue orb. Waves of psychic energy pulsed outward, dragging the air into it like a black hole.

"If you won't listen—I'll just take you down first!"

Mewtwo hurled the orb.

The red Genesect felt mortal danger and tried to use Extreme Speed—but this attack followed it.

Psystrike!

Mewtwo's signature move!

The Genesect turned into a saucer and bolted upward.

The orb followed.

So did Mewtwo.

Leaving behind a stunned crowd of humans and Pokémon.

"So... scary..."

Mewtwo frowned. The Psystrike wasn't connecting.

That Genesect was fast—faster than even Mewtwo.

Should it use that power?

In mid-flight, Mewtwo summoned a Mega Stone—it could feel its energy, belonging to itself.

But the key to unlock it was missing.

It could force the evolution—but it wasn't confident in controlling the result.

Still...

It was considering it when Johan's voice came again:

Mewtwo, if you can't catch it—try Future Sight.

Future Sight?

Mewtwo's eyes gleamed.

Right!

Its vision shimmered, bending time and space—it saw the Genesect's path.

Couldn't catch it? Then predict it!

Johan was clever. He understood tactics and misdirection—more than Mewtwo's brute force instincts.

"Got you."

Mewtwo locked on.

The Psystrike orb curved mid-air.

The red Genesect realized too late—

BOOOOM!!

A psychic explosion tore the sky apart, blasting clouds into vapor.

The red Genesect tumbled down.

——

Back at the sanctuary:

The Ice Drive Genesect held down the Electric one.

Dragonite pounded the Fire Drive with bullet fists. Tinkaton swung her hammer.

"DRAAA!! (Ura Ura Ura—!!)"

"Cha! Cha!" (Calm down! Calm down!!)

The Water Drive Genesect was caught in a full nelson by Urshifu.

Armarouge, now Ceruledge again, slashed repeatedly, blue fire trailing each cut.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar grinned, licking its tongue ominously as it crept toward the terrified Electric Genesect.

Even the wild Pokémon nearby shivered.

The Genesects' feet and cannons had been coated in hard frost—not enough to stop them, but enough to slow them.

Five Genesects had been overwhelming for Johan.

But with the red one gone and the Ice one flipped—only two remained.

That was manageable.

Johan adjusted his monocle. Earlier, he'd felt the Mega Stone chain heat up—but it faded quickly. He'd thought Gengar wanted to Mega Evolve, but now wasn't the time to worry about it.

"Alright, Gengar, Ninetales—prepare for surgical sleep."

"Ka-kaah!"

"Oowoo~"

Gengar grinned wider, casting Dark Void. Ninetales' eyes shimmered with psychic glow.

While brute force was useful, hypnosis had its place.

Then—

The red glow faded from the Genesects' eyes.

Now's the chance!

"Now!"

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar fired. Ninetales amplified it. Johan channeled Aura.

Water Drive Genesect—down.

Electric Drive—down.

"Stop, Tinkaton, Dragonite!"

They halted.

The Fire Drive, aching all over, gratefully passed out.

Three Genesects—asleep.

Johan focused his Aura to project an image—of rocky spires, green fields, shimmering lakes filled with Aurus flowers.

A home.

The Genesects' resistance melted.

This was home.

Whew—

Johan wiped his brow.

It was done.

"Rotom, find the control devices. Remove them."

Though freed from Colress, the control implants were still there.

Could be reactivated.

And Johan wasn't sure if the red leader's control was due to his status—or the implants.

Rotom zipped into the slots in their backs, starting the removal.

Then—

A streak of light landed in front of them.

Mewtwo.

Holding the red Genesect leader—unconscious.

"You got it?" Johan asked.

Mewtwo met his eyes.

"I am Mewtwo."

Its voice echoed in everyone's minds.

But to Johan, the meaning was deeper.

Who am I?

Mewtwo.

Thanks to Johan, it had found its identity. Its self.

To a Pokémon psychologist like Johan, that was the greatest success possible.

Only one thing left to worry about:

What would Mewtwo do when it met the "chubby" original—Mew?

Johan chuckled quietly.

"Ka-kaah!"

I'm Gengar!

Gengar thought Mewtwo was greeting it and waved.

"I know you, Gastly," Mewtwo nodded.

"Ka-kaah?"

Huh?

Gengar scratched its head.

Why did everyone keep calling it Gastly lately?

Then—

"Watch out!"

The Ice Drive Genesect warned.

The red leader—was awake!

Or maybe… it never was unconscious.

It fired a beam—

High-Tech Cannon!

Target: Johan.

It had planned to fake its loss, rejoin its team—but returned to find them all unconscious.

Clearly, Johan's doing.

Its rage exploded.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar leapt in front of Johan. Ninetales followed.

But before the beam could hit—

Mewtwo stepped forward.

One hand raised—Psychic shield engaged.

The blast met resistance.

BOOOOM!!

A shockwave tore through the earth.

Johan felt the Mega Stone burn again—

Then fade.

Gengar and Ninetales were at Mewtwo's side, holding the line.

Dragonite, Tinkaton, Urshifu, and Ceruledge all roared and charged toward the red Genesect once again.

This was the final straw.

Enough was enough.

Again and again, this Genesect had tried to harm Johan. Even after being captured. Even after being spared.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar's fury boiled over. It had always been playful, but now, it looked truly terrifying—shadowy aura swirling like a storm.

"Oowoo!"

Ninetales let out a piercing cry, icy mist exploding around it. The temperature dropped sharply.

Dragonite struck first.

BAM!

A spinning tail-whip sent the red Genesect flying into the sanctuary's outer wall. The structure groaned, but held.

Urshifu caught up midair—grappling it, locking both arms, and flipping it into the ground with a thunderous suplex.

"Goka!"

Ceruledge slashed downwards with its twin blades, each blow embedding searing blue fire deeper into the Genesect's chassis.

Tinkaton raised her hammer high—charging, glowing—only for Johan to shout:

"Stop!"

She halted inches from contact. The Genesect's cannon powered down. Its limbs twitched, but it didn't move to attack.

Johan approached, eyes glowing with Aura.

"Mewtwo, now. Help me reach it."

"Understood."

Johan and Mewtwo's minds linked, and their combined psychic and aura energy pierced into the inner psyche of the red Genesect.

It was a chaos of images.

Pain. Cages. Wires. Commands. Darkness. Testing. Orders. Ice. Fire. Fighting.

A cold, metallic voice.

You exist to fight. To obey. To purge.

In this swirling void, Johan's voice emerged like a ripple in still water:

"Is this really what you want?"

A flicker.

More memories. More pain.

Then—one faint spark.

Green fields. Blue sky. A feeling not from its current self—but something ancient. From its fossil. From the time it lived freely.

The real home.

Johan pressed on.

"I don't want to control you. I want to give you a choice."

Mewtwo's energy steadied the mental chaos.

Slowly, the red Genesect's consciousness began to stabilize.

The cannon powered down fully.

Its red eyes faded to a dull glow.

"...Choice?"

The voice came not in the mind—but out loud.

It looked up at Johan, blinking slowly.

"I don't trust humans," it rasped. "But... I don't want to hurt them anymore either."

Johan nodded.

"That's good enough for me."

The Pokémon behind Johan—all panting, ready to strike again—held back.

Mewtwo turned away, arms crossed. "If it breaks its word, I'll know."

The red Genesect said nothing, but lowered its head.

A long silence followed.

Then, softly:

"...Where is home?"

Johan smiled, raising the Aurus flower in his hand.

"Let's go find it."

# 523: Mewtwo's Mega Evolution Stone

Johan rarely leaves his Pokémon with bad dreams anymore—or rather, unpleasant "experiences."

On one hand, doing so can leave a mental shadow, and unless the Pokémon is truly malicious, it feels wrong.

On the other hand, the truly dangerous ones Johan meets now are so powerful that hypnotizing them is no easy feat.

Just like the red Genesect in front of him.

But if you add Mewtwo's psychic power and let Johan take the lead, then yes—he could "arrange" something.

Hum—

The red Genesect suddenly felt like the world around it had shifted.

It was still itself, but its role had switched with that of the Feraligatr, Houndoom, and Persian.

The powerful "Three Kings"—Feraligatr, Houndoom, and Persian—had invaded their home.

Why?

Because those "Three Kings" had lost their original home.

And the home the Genesect called theirs now?

The invaders liked it.

It felt like "home" to them.

So they pushed the Genesect out.

"If you get close, you're an obstacle.

Obstacles must be eliminated!"

The red Genesect felt completely aggrieved.

"Why do we have to lose our home just because you lost yours?!" it roared.

But halfway through, it froze.

That phrase…

Sounded all too familiar.

"If you've lost your home, we can share."

"Yes, we can live together—just don't destroy ours!"

"How are you any different from the ones who destroyed your old home?"

The shouts from its imagined companions made the red Genesect's pupils shrink.

Hum—

Another swap.

It was back in its original role, facing the Three Kings—Feraligatr, Houndoom, and Persian—now the ones roaring in protest.

As it floated, issuing its command to clear the area, the red Genesect hesitated.

Should it attack… or not?

Then the illusion changed again.

This time, it wasn't just the Three Kings in front of it.

"Hiss—"

The red Genesect gasped.

Towering before it were legends:

The green serpent of the skies.

The monstrous dragon from the World's End.

The twin gods of time and space.

Ho-Oh.

Lugia.

Mewtwo.

And in the center, calm and commanding—

Johan.

A cold, amused smile crept across Johan's face.

He waved.

"Get him."

That moment was its last chance.

If the red Genesect had chosen to stop, it would've been spared.

But even a moment's hesitation?

Too late.

Johan was never one to be merciful.

You liked to bully the weak?

He'd show you what real power meant.

"Sorry, but I'm petty like that," Johan smirked.

Even if the Genesect had confidence in its strength…

Confidence doesn't block laser beams.

In the real world, while Mewtwo, Gengar, and Ninetales had intercepted its ambush, and the others were charging in with fury—

The red Genesect suddenly twitched violently, eyes rolling back as it collapsed.

Its body spasmed.

Foam trickled from its jaws.

"…Huh?"

The wild Pokémon blinked in confusion.

Only Gengar seemed to understand.

He grinned wickedly, stuck his tongue out, and whispered:

"Ka-kaah~"

(That's what you get.)

Mewtwo withdrew its psychic energy and looked at Johan, intrigued.

It had participated in Johan's dream sequence…

And even though Genesect lacked psychic powers, its willpower should've been strong enough to tell illusion from reality.

But the dream had been too real.

Way too real.

The Rayquaza.

Giratina.

The creation gods.

Lugia.

Ho-Oh.

Even Mewtwo had felt their auras radiating from Johan.

How?

It began to suspect—

Maybe Johan had really encountered them.

Mewtwo glanced at him again.

Wasn't it supposed to be the most powerful?

A flicker of doubt crossed its thoughts.

"Gengar, take its cassette out," Johan ordered.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar's eyes lit up as he happily skipped to the fallen Genesect, yanking the golden cassette from its back.

He examined it.

This little thing was the source of those high-powered blasts?

He tried to find a slot on his own body to install it.

Maybe the mouth?

…or the butt?

Nah, no good.

No cassette = no high-tech cannon.

Even if it woke up, the red Genesect would be severely weakened.

"Is it… cured?" Mewtwo asked.

"That depends," Johan said.

"On whether it understood."

"Understood what?"

"Don't do unto others what you don't want done to you."

Mewtwo repeated it quietly.

"…That's a good line."

"Oh right—why did you suddenly show up?" Johan asked.

Truth be told, he hadn't expected Mewtwo.

His original plan was to stall until Cynthia and Caitlin arrived, then fight together.

Mewtwo nodded. "I foresaw that you might face trouble. So I awakened early."

Its tone remained flat, emotionless—robotic even.

But Johan caught the care buried within.

"And…" Mewtwo added, "I wanted to see these Pokémon.

I feel… they are like me."

Johan blinked.

They did share something.

Both were resurrected from fossilized genetics.

One was rebuilt by Plasma's twisted science.

The other was born from Mew's DNA mixed with others.

But—Johan smiled—

"No. You're Mewtwo. You're not like them."

He extended a hand.

"Hi, Mewtwo. I'm Johan."

Mewtwo stared at the hand for a second.

Then…

Its lips curved.

A faint smile.

A first.

And it shook Johan's hand.

If Team Rocket's scientists saw this, they'd flip.

Mewtwo just smiled?!

In their minds, Mewtwo was always angry, always hostile.

But happy? Smiling?

Unthinkable.

Hum—

Just then, Johan's Key Stone heated again.

Earlier, he'd thought it was responding to Gengar.

But now?

His gaze slowly turned to Mewtwo.

Mewtwo, puzzled, looked back.

Then with a flick of its wrist, it summoned something:

Its own Mega Stone.

Mewtwonite Y.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar blinked, then spat out his own Mega Stone. He compared the two.

They weren't the same.

"Wait…" Johan muttered, stunned.

In the anime, Mewtwo could Mega Evolve freely—like Rayquaza.

In the games, it had two Mega Evolutions—X and Y.

Only Charizard had the same dual privilege.

But now, Mewtwo held a unique Mega Stone.

And—

It reacted to Johan's Key Stone.

Was it possible…

Could he trigger Mewtwo's Mega Evolution?

Johan felt a surge of possibility.

A spark of destiny.

This was no ordinary partnership.

Mewtwo simply thought:

Johan… is my key.

# 524: Want to Learn? I'll Teach You

The three Genesect slowly awoke.

When they looked at Johan again, their expressions had softened significantly.

Although Johan couldn't prove he'd definitely help them return to their original home, he'd at least shown them the vision they longed for. That was enough for them to hold onto hope.

If they could "go home" without stealing someone else's, they'd much rather avoid hurting others.

"I'll find you a home," Johan promised again.

In the original story, the Genesect eventually found a place similar to their long-lost homeland. But searching for such a place might take time.

And if he couldn't find one?

Then he'd build a simulated habitat for them!

There was no way the Unova League wouldn't be willing to support five Genesect. And if not, the Hoenn or Sinnoh League surely would.

Johan believed in his ability to get support—from Steven and Devon Corporation, if necessary.

He had no qualms about making the promise.

"I believe you," the Ice Drive Genesect said, now free of the red chain, yet still the first to express trust in Johan.

The other three looked at each other, hesitated slightly, then nodded slowly.

They were used to following commands, not making decisions for themselves. But Johan didn't mind.

"I'll help you learn to think for yourselves," he said with a smile.

"I said earlier I'd feed everyone…"

So Johan made a generous offering—distributing plenty of Pokéblocks, not just for the Pokémon living at the sanctuary but for the four Genesect too.

"Geno, Geno!"

Even the local Pokémon were won over by the delicious Pokéblocks—let alone the Genesect, who had never tasted such things. Three hundred million years ago, this kind of food didn't exist.

During their time under Colress's control, they had only been given nutrient fluids.

So when they tried these tasty, novel snacks?

They were instantly conquered.

Their distrust of Johan vanished.

They weren't fools—capable of telepathic communication, they could sense Johan's kindness through his aura. His actions confirmed it too.

As for the local Pokémon, Johan and his team had defended their home. Though some had been hurt, and the Genesect's earlier actions caused discomfort, they could understand.

Still, the Genesect were subtly ostracized—Johan noticed how they were slightly isolated when he handed out snacks.

Thankfully, with Nurse Joy and Chansey tending to the injured, tensions were reduced. With help from Gengar, Ceruledge, and Larvitar, the others started to let their guard down.

A Leavanny, after seeing the Genesect weren't full, hesitated, then quietly placed two berries beside them before darting away.

"Geno…"

The Genesect stared at the berries in stunned silence.

Then a Sawsbuck and a Beautifly, sensing the awkwardness, each contributed a berry as well.

Four berries.

The Genesect, already guilt-ridden, felt even worse.

Then they bowed.

"Geno! Geno!"

We're sorry!

"Ka-kaah~ Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar suddenly popped up beside Ice Drive Genesect, laughing as he clapped its shoulder.

"Yoji?"

Larvitar crouched near the berries, glancing from Genesect to the snacks.

You guys not gonna eat these? 'Cause if not... I will!

Despite lingering unease, the sincere apology touched everyone—and the Genesect were forgiven.

Pokémon could tell kindness from malice.

Still, Johan clapped his hands to draw their attention.

"If you made a mistake, you'll be punished."

"Geno." The four nodded earnestly.

Some sanctuary Pokémon looked hesitant—they'd already apologized.

Johan just smiled.

"Then your punishment... is to help rebuild the sanctuary!"

Though he'd tried to keep the fight outside, the damage was still noticeable.

"Geno! Geno!"

Excited by the chance to redeem themselves, the Genesect were full of energy.

Together with the others, they worked tirelessly—and slowly earned full forgiveness.

Well, for those four, anyway.

From a hillside, Johan watched with satisfaction.

"So this... is what Pokémon are like?"

Mewtwo had appeared beside him.

It had watched silently from the sidelines since the battle ended. Now it finally spoke.

"Pretty simple, right?" Johan replied.

Mewtwo nodded. Simple—in a good way.

Having seen the complexity of humans, it found this refreshing.

"No one tried to stop you from coming out?"

Johan was honestly surprised.

No reaction from Team Rocket?

"No one can stop me." Mewtwo's tone was flat, but the pride was unmistakable.

Tch.

"Porygon-Z covered for me," it added.

That made sense.

"So, that Mega Stone of yours…"

"This one?"

Mewtwo held out its Mega Stone Y, its power clear to the senses.

It explained:

During the battle between Deoxys and Rayquaza, Mewtwo had, through a data link from Porygon-Z, absorbed some of the life energy that drove Deoxys berserk.

That energy fused with Mewtwo's own power, forming the Mega Stone Y.

Johan once again felt his Key Stone react to Mewtwo's stone.

Could he Mega Evolve Mewtwo?

The thought was thrilling.

But… could he even handle it?

They weren't officially bonded.

But Johan knew—capturing wasn't a requirement for Mega Evolution.

"Oh right."

He took out a box of Pokéblocks and macarons, offering them to Mewtwo.

"Saw you didn't eat earlier. Not sure which ones you'd like, so… here's all of them."

He smiled at the busy Pokémon around them.

"I've had these ready for a while. Figured you'd like them."

Psychic-types loved certain flavors. With Gardevoir and Gallade at home, Johan always had some on hand.

Mewtwo turned to glance at the stream.

Not gonna take it?

Or embarrassed?

Then—whoosh—a psychic force lifted the snacks from Johan's hand and floated them to Mewtwo, who still had its back turned.

Johan rubbed his chin.

Was that… tsundere behavior?

"Mmm…"

The red Genesect leader clutched its head and stood up shakily.

It opened its eyes… and saw Johan smiling kindly.

It froze.

Backpedaled several steps.

Flashes of the "dream" surged through its mind. It shuddered.

"Ge-Geno?"

Wh-what do you want?

Then the other Pokémon and the four Genesect surrounded it.

Seeing the locals wasn't scary.

But seeing its own comrades standing with Johan?

The Genesect leader broke.

Johan stepped forward.

It stepped back.

He nodded in satisfaction.

Perfect.

Psychological damage: confirmed.

"Still want to take over this place?" Johan asked, smiling with that "nuclear kindness."

The Genesect flinched.

That same smile… was the one he wore in the dream. Right before ten legendaries jumped it.

A totally unfair, full-team beatdown.

And they didn't hold back.

It shook its head quickly. Stepped back again.

Johan grinned wider.

Excellent.

Some people only understand strength. It thought it could act just because it was strong.

Now? It had been humbled.

"My offer stands. I'll help you find a home. The choice is yours."

The Genesect leader looked like it wanted to cry.

"Th-thank you…" it said telepathically.

Before it could finish—

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar popped up behind it, red eyes glowing.

"You said what now? Say it again, pal."

Hum—

Mewtwo appeared beside Gengar, arms crossed, giving it a side-eye like, You sure about that?

"…H-hoping… to find a home," it replied nervously.

Gengar snorted.

"Ka-kaah~~"

I liked you better when you were rebellious. Think you can go back to that?

The Genesect said nothing.

Thus ended the Genesect incident.

Now, Johan would help them find—or build—a home.

As long as they behaved.

And they would.

They had no cassette drives. No control. And no will left to resist.

The sun dipped toward the horizon.

The Pokémon Sanctuary was slowly being rebuilt.

Mewtwo leaned against a tree branch, arms folded, watching the busy Pokémon with an unreadable expression.

It flipped its hand—holding a Pokéblock.

It studied it for a moment, about to eat it when—

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar peeked out of the tree's shadow, staring curiously at Mewtwo.

It looked familiar somehow.

But Mewtwo noticed. It paused, hid the snack, and looked over.

"Gastly."

"Ka-kaah!"

I'm Gengar! Gengar!

Mewtwo nodded.

"Alright, Gastly."

"…."

Gengar scratched its head. Didn't know what to say.

"You're not bad," Mewtwo added.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar perked up. Was that a compliment?

"But your ability lacks flexibility," Mewtwo continued.

"Ka-kaah?"

Flexibility?

"Like this."

Mewtwo lifted a hand, three fingers spreading. Psychic and spiritual energy swirled together.

Gengar leaned closer.

It looked like a special attack… but something felt off.

It felt… physical?

If Johan were here, he'd recognize it immediately.

Mewtwo's exclusive move: Psystrike.

A special attack that calculated damage using the opponent's physical defense.

In reality, its properties were even stranger.

Gengar's eyes lit up.

Mewtwo smirked.

"Want to learn it? I'll teach you."

# 525: This Gengar Is Ridiculous

"Ka-kaah~~"

In a quiet corner of the Pokémon Sanctuary, Gengar carefully kneaded together a large black orb.

Wisps of Ghost-type energy gathered and swirled within the sphere.

Huh?

Mewtwo, watching from nearby, looked mildly surprised.

Sure, it had known Gengar was powerful—but this was the first time it had seen Gengar form such a large Shadow Ball.

From behind a tree, a Dusknoir peeked its head out and silently contributed some of its Ghost-type energy to the process.

But that wasn't the point.

The important thing was—

Mewtwo was trying to get Gengar to transform its Special Attack-oriented move into something more physical in nature.

Mewtwo once again gathered a sphere of intertwined psychic and mental energy, showcasing the properties of Psystrike.

Gengar watched intently, learning as it went.

And honestly—

Mewtwo was once again surprised.

Gengar actually showed a bit of talent for it!

Before long, it had already started to get the feel of it.

Was my move always this easy? Mewtwo couldn't help but wonder.

It didn't know—

Johan's Gengar had always preferred physical attacks. Punching was just the basics—what Gengar truly loved were mischievous, ghostly physical techniques.

So for a move like Psystrike, which visually looked like a special attack but used physical power, Gengar didn't necessarily master it instantly—but it definitely grasped the core concept fast.

Johan's guidance in letting Gengar develop physical moves had once again paid off.

Mewtwo was genuinely impressed with Gengar's speed of learning.

As Johan passed by—having finished organizing both the Sanctuary Pokémon and the Genesect—he caught sight of Gengar learning from Mewtwo.

He paused.

When did my Ghost/Poison-type start learning from a Psychic-type like Mewtwo?

And… was that Psystrike?!

Wait a second—

He knew his Gengar well.

Though Gengar was a Ghost-type, due to its "age," its mental strength wasn't weak. But learning Psystrike?

Actually… it kinda looks like it's working?

Mewtwo used psychic and mental force.

But Gengar was using ghostly energy and mental force.

It wasn't so much learning Psystrike, as learning the technique behind it.

"Good lord… when the 'Youth Masters World Championship' comes up, is my Gengar going to get banned?"

Because some of Gengar's moves were straight-up ridiculous.

It had learned Dark Void from Darkrai, Spectral Thief from Marshadow, a unique form of Shadow Sneak from Giratina and the space-time rift…

And now? It was learning Psystrike's physical conversion concept from Mewtwo.

"This Gengar… is getting more and more busted."

Maybe he should save Gengar for the final match?

What if it got banned after one fight?

But then again—

None of the other participants—like Lance, Steven, or Cynthia—were exactly bringing "reasonable" Pokémon either.

He wouldn't even be surprised if some legendaries showed up.

Speaking of which—

Johan glanced at Mewtwo and smirked.

"I just hope everyone stays 'reasonable.' Otherwise, I might flip the table."

—

Night fell.

Power hadn't fully returned to the Pokémon Sanctuary yet, but out in the fields, orange firelight flickered.

A bonfire blazed on the grass.

All the Pokémon—those from the Sanctuary, Johan's own, and the Genesect—gathered there.

All but one.

The "pathetic, weak, helpless, pitiful" Genesect leader sat alone.

Mewtwo had once again perched in a tree, silently observing.

It didn't know how to join in… but it liked the atmosphere.

"Looks… lively."

Cynthia and Caitlin finally arrived at the Sanctuary outside Nuvema City.

From a distance, they saw the lively bonfire scene.

Cynthia gently tucked her flowing golden hair behind her ear and smiled.

But Caitlin, who had been bouncing happily a moment ago, now wore a serious expression.

"What's wrong?"

Cynthia noticed the change.

Caitlin pressed her lips together, hesitated, and then said, "Such powerful psychic energy…"

Huh?

Cynthia was stunned.

She'd never heard Caitlin say that before.

As a top-tier psychic in the world of Pokémon, Caitlin ranked easily in the top three. For her to be impressed?

"You're here?"

Johan walked over, smiling.

"Looks like you've handled the problem?" Cynthia asked with a smile.

"More or less."

The Genesect had been "calmed" for now. Next step: help them find a home.

Which, now that he thought about it, might be something to discuss with Cynthia.

"More or less?"

Cynthia didn't press for details—Johan looked relaxed, so the issue seemed under control.

"What's with her?" Johan asked, noticing Caitlin's dazed look.

"No clue," Cynthia replied. "She was fine earlier, but after we arrived at the Sanctuary, she said she sensed strong psychic power?"

Strong psychic power…

Johan immediately understood.

His expression turned oddly amused.

Caitlin had probably sensed Mewtwo—or rather, Mewtwo's psychic aura.

Caitlin had always been proud of her powers. Now she'd met her match.

"Makes sense. Don't worry, it's nothing." Johan replied with a sly smile.

Cynthia squinted at him.

Why do I feel like Johan's messing with her?

"By the way, what exactly happened?"

Caitlin had told her bits and pieces, but secondhand info only went so far.

"Well…"

Johan recounted everything—from Team Plasma, to the Genesect, to Kyurem.

By the end, Cynthia's face had turned serious too.

"I thought it was this guy."

She showed Johan a news article—with photos.

The headline:

"Shocking sighting! Never-before-seen powerful Pokémon, suspected to be a Legendary!"

Photos: Mewtwo.

"Looks like two were fighting? But that cat-like Pokémon doesn't seem hostile to humans?"

Johan's eye twitched at the comments.

Well great.

Mewtwo was famous now.

At least it wasn't infamous.

Wait…

If people found out Mewtwo was with him, would he get labeled "that guy with all the legendaries" again?

"Who are they?"

Meanwhile, from his perch outside the Sanctuary, Mewtwo had noticed Cynthia and Caitlin.

He asked aloud.

Gengar popped out from the shadows beside him, munching roasted corn. It offered Mewtwo a piece.

"Ka-kaah?"

Want some?

The smell of roasted corn wafted through the air.

Then Gengar looked toward Johan and the others. Its eyes lit up.

"Ka-kaah!"

Cynthia!

Cynthia?

Gengar, corn in mouth, made a mischievous little gesture with its thumbs—grinning at Mewtwo like, You get it?

Mewtwo: "…"

Get what?

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar pointed at the dazed Caitlin.

That's the little psychic witch.

Mewtwo's eyes settled on Caitlin.

She tensed up instinctively.

"Impressive psychic power," Mewtwo nodded slightly.

"Ka-kaah~"

Yep, pretty good~ Gengar agreed.

Then it paused.

It looked at its hands.

Wait.

Where's the second corn skewer it brought?

Gengar looked sideways at Mewtwo.

Mewtwo's expression was blank.

Gengar leaned in.

There, at the corner of Mewtwo's mouth… a faint smudge from the roasted corn.

"Ka-kaah!" Gengar yelped.

Mewtwo: "…"

And vanished.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar scratched its head, confused.

What? I brought it for you anyway—why so shy?

—

"Nurse Joy, where was this photo taken?"

Inside a corridor of the Sanctuary, Nurse Joy stopped as Johan, Cynthia, and Caitlin followed.

On the wall was a landscape photo.

"High art," Rotom chimed. As an expert in photography, I must say—

This was the photo Johan had been searching for.

Floating in the lake were the flowers the Genesect loved—Aorta Blossoms.

"That one…"

Nurse Joy thought for a moment.

"It was taken in a remote mountain forest. Not sure where exactly, but it's in the northern-central part of Unova."

Johan quietly made note of the location.

If he promised to help the Genesect find a home, then he'd follow through.

And frankly, a place away from humans might suit them better.

—

In the foggy Reverse Mountain of Unova

A massive black battleship hovered among the clouds.

In front of a huge projection screen stood Colress, cradling his mini-computer, a calm smile on his face.

On the screen: Ghetsis, one of the Seven Sages and N's adoptive father.

"If you believe it's necessary, let them assist you," Ghetsis said coldly.

Team Plasma was in trouble.

But Ghetsis didn't seem worried.

Colress smiled wider and bowed.

"Thank you."

Ghetsis nodded faintly.

"Their 'sacrifice' was inevitable, but they are still our troops. Make it worth something."

"Absolutely!"

Colress glanced toward a ship chamber—something terrifying was awakening.

"This is our chance."

The League wanted to arrest them?

Let them.

Sacrifice a few pawns. Reap the reward.

And if the Unova League thought they'd destroyed Team Plasma?

Good.

That's when the real plan would begin.

Colress ended the call—and immediately contacted another Sage: Zinzolin.

"Prepare. We move now."

His tone was calm.

But behind his glasses, his eyes gleamed with madness.

Target—DNA Splicers.

—

At N's Castle

Ghetsis stood before the grand window, hands on his staff, gazing into the distance.

He wasn't worried about Colress.

No one but Team Plasma could satisfy his twisted needs.

But one person did worry him.

His foster son—N.

"Lord Ghetsis, Lord N has returned."

A grunt entered, respectful.

N was technically the king of Team Plasma, but they still deferred to Ghetsis—because he was N's "father."

"Tell him I'm not here."

Ghetsis walked toward the secret chamber.

"Yes, sir!"

No one was surprised.

He'd been refusing to see N ever since his return.

To most people, it looked like he was angry at N for vanishing for half a year.

But the truth?

Ghetsis wasn't mad.

N was just a puppet.

Someone to be thrown to the League if things went south.

Once the League thought they'd captured the leader?

Ghetsis would vanish—into the shadows—ready to rebuild again.

The League was closing in.

N's time was running out.

And Ghetsis?

Had no intention of seeing a soon-to-be criminal with a silly idea like "Pokémon liberation."

# 526: Professor Johan’s “Release”

The weather in April was noticeably warmer than in March.

It was another quiet evening.

Johan and the others had already left the Pokémon Sanctuary and arrived at a remote forest in central Unova.

Based on Nurse Joy's description and further research by Porygon-Z, they had finally pinpointed this place.

Beside a softly babbling lake nestled in the grassy clearing of the forest—

Johan, wearing an apron, was barbecuing in the open air.

Sizzling meat turned golden over the fire, fat dripping onto the coals with a hiss. As he sprinkled cumin and chili powder, the aroma filled the air.

Evening breezes danced across the lake, stirring the surface and releasing the fragrance of the Aorta Blossoms floating on the water—an aroma that made even Gengar drool.

The sun hadn't fully set, but the moon had already risen eagerly. One side of the sky glowed golden, while the other revealed a slice of the starry cosmos.

Cynthia sat elegantly at a table covered in white cloth, crossing her legs and sipping fruit juice through a straw with a soft smile.

Next to her, Caitlin looked rather awkward, fidgeting with her knees and stealing glances at Mewtwo—who also wore an apron, its expression that of deep existential regret.

The stronger a psychic user was, the more acutely they could feel the overwhelming presence radiating from Mewtwo.

It wasn't that Caitlin thought she was completely powerless against it—but she had never felt such a powerful psychic force before, and she wasn't sure what Mewtwo's intent was.

Even Cynthia's Garchomp had unconsciously tempered its presence after seeing Mewtwo.

Especially with Mewtwo currently looking like a ticking time bomb.

And whose fault was that? Johan's.

All because he wanted Mewtwo to "experience life," and forced it to wear a cute, pink Jigglypuff-themed apron.

Hence, the expression of eternal despair.

The Genesect also sensed the awkward tension.

But not Johan and his Pokémon, or Cynthia. They actually found it kind of cute.

"See? Doesn't it look nice? All soft and pink," Johan teased.

Mewtwo: "…"

Caitlin stared at Johan in disbelief.

How does he dare?!

But Johan wasn't reckless—he just knew that while Mewtwo might get annoyed, it wouldn't actually snap.

Mewtwo still struggled with its identity. Was it a Pokémon? A human? Something in between?

So why not help it experience things?

Even embarrassment was an emotion worth learning.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar, also wearing an apron, cheerfully patted Mewtwo's shoulder, grinning ear to ear.

Just like me, pal. Let's be good little waiters together. Ka-kaah-ka-kaah~~

He proudly tugged on his custom Gengar apron, feeling fabulous.

Mewtwo shot him a side-eye. A calm thought echoed in Gengar's mind:

"One hundred Shadow Ball drills tonight."

Gengar: Σ(дlll)

Mewtwo smirked slightly—but quickly composed itself.

"Yoji~ Yoji~~"

Nearby, Larvitar was swinging on a swing made of Genesect silk.

It burst into laughter at the scene—then promptly fell off.

But it was tough, bounced up, and climbed right back on.

"Alright, food's ready!"

Johan clapped his hands and called the Genesect over.

"Once you eat, you'll be on your way."

Genesect Leader: Σ(дlll)

Wait—what do you mean "on our way"? Are you gonna kill us?

"Ahem. I mean, once you eat, you'll be homebound," Johan corrected with a cough.

This wasn't their original home—but like the Pokémon Sanctuary, it had Aorta Blossoms, the flowers they yearned for.

It was far from human civilization. As long as they didn't cause trouble, they could live here for hundreds of years in peace.

"Remember—no stirring up trouble. I'll be watching," Johan said to the Genesect Leader.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar made a "watching you" gesture.

The Leader didn't react—but the other four Genesect nodded rapidly behind it.

Genesect Leader: "…"

It looked up at a forty-five-degree angle, eyes misting over.

Why did it feel like Johan was their real leader?

They'd grown up together—it had always been the one in charge!

Is this what they mean by "childhood friend loses to the new arrival"?

Still, with a home secured and Johan's earlier dreamscape still lingering in its mind, the Genesect Leader had no desire to stir trouble again.

"Xiao Bing, you've got my contact info. If anything comes up, get in touch."

"Gene, Gene!"

The Frost Drive Genesect nodded vigorously.

"Dig in!"

Johan brought over the grilled meat.

Whoosh—

Suddenly, the cute pink apron fluttered to the ground.

Mewtwo, having fulfilled its "mission," vanished instantly.

"Shy little thing," Cynthia chuckled, taking a bite of lamb skewer.

"It's fine," Johan waved.

It wasn't just embarrassment.

Mewtwo still didn't quite know how to interact with others—whether Pokémon or human.

And the missing plate of grilled meat on the table was proof enough.

"Seems lonely," Cynthia commented.

That's also why she didn't fear Mewtwo losing control.

Lonely beings often looked aloof or annoyed—but inside…

Johan stirred his juice quietly and said, "It's lost a lot of companions."

He looked up at the sky in the direction Mewtwo flew off.

"But I'll help it find more."

Caitlin, seeing it fly off, asked, "Will it lose companions again?"

"It's strong…"

"No one has smooth sailing forever," Johan replied.

Cynthia and Caitlin exchanged looks—then nodded.

True.

"Anyway." Caitlin changed the heavy topic. "This is the first time I've seen—or even heard—of it."

The name Mewtwo, plus its appearance, made it hard not to think of Mew.

"Its origin…"

Johan didn't finish the sentence. He changed the subject.

"No need to talk about that. Come on—eat up! I put a lot of love into this BBQ!"

—

Meanwhile—Unova, Nacrene City.

A city transformed from hundred-year-old warehouses—now full of historic charm and modern flair.

Its pride: the Nacrene Museum.

Visitors seeking Unova's history always stopped there.

But today—

Even though it was nearly closing time, the museum was packed.

Until—

BOOM!!!

A massive explosion rocked the museum, shaking all of Nacrene City.

Officer Jenny, long prepared, led her squad into action.

Lenora, Gym Leader and historian, stepped from a hidden chamber.

Inside the museum—

Masked, uniformed individuals ransacked the exhibits.

They summoned their Pokémon but didn't attack—waiting.

Team Plasma.

"Damn it! It's not here!"

"No meteorite!"

"We've been duped!"

Their goal: a mysterious meteorite said to influence or control Pokémon.

But Officer Jenny had already hidden it in advance—having received intel about the heist.

"Don't panic!" barked a Team Plasma commander.

They finally noticed Jenny and Lenora—but didn't look worried.

Instead, the commander smirked.

"They must know where it is."

"Surrender now!" Jenny shouted, her Arcanine poised.

"No—you surrender!" Plasma's commander sneered.

Footsteps echoed.

Jenny and Lenora's faces changed.

They'd been baited.

More Plasma grunts outside.

So many…

For just a meteorite?

Or…

Was it all a diversion?

Jenny and Lenora alerted the Unova League immediately.

Hearing the news, League members mobilized across Unova.

Team Plasma wouldn't escape.

—

"Team Plasma's attacking Nacrene? For a meteorite?"

Johan and the group, having just "released" the Genesect, also got word.

Do I have the right to "release" now? Johan mused.

"What meteorite?" Cynthia asked.

"One that can influence a Pokémon's heart."

Johan remembered it—Team Plasma and Team Rocket once fought over it in the original story.

Sure, it was useful—but…

"Is this really worth all the noise?"

Others might not know—but Johan did.

The meteorite wasn't that important to Colress's research.

Unless—

They already had Kyurem.

That would explain it.

But… to bait the League just to ambush them?

That's bold—even for Colress.

If Giovanni were the leader, sure. But Ghetsis?

A man too cowardly to even show his face—pushing his foster son N to the front.

He was a schemer—not a warlord.

"It's far…"

Caitlin checked her phone. "Do we go?"

Even if they rushed, they'd probably be late.

Unless—

Mewtwo could be the key.

Should they call the Genesect back?

They might still make it.

Johan sighed—traveling like that again…

Then—

A thought struck him.

What if the meteorite wasn't even the real target?

What if it was all a distraction?

What did Team Plasma really want?

Zekrom and Reshiram.

But those dragons couldn't be captured so easily.

Unless—

Johan suddenly looked north.

Opelucid City. The DNA Splicers.

Decoy tactics?

It all fit.

Maybe Nacrene was a smokescreen.

Their real target—

The DNA Splicers.

—

At the same time, in the shadows of night—

A massive battleship approached Opelucid.

Inside the control room, Colress pushed his glasses up.

The excitement in his eyes—barely contained.
